{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/cli/src/installers/safe-paths.ts",
  "content": "import { resolve, sep } from \"path\";\n\n// Mirrors shared/validators/manifest.ts SLUG_PATTERN. Duplicated here to\n// keep the CLI binary self-contained (no cross-package imports into the\n// Nuxt app). If the canonical pattern changes, update both.\nconst SLUG_PATTERN = /^[a-z0-9]+(?:-[a-z0-9]+)*$/;\n\nexport function assertValidSlug(slug: string): void {\n  if (!SLUG_PATTERN.test(slug)) {\n    throw new Error(`Invalid slug: ${JSON.stringify(slug)}`);\n  }\n}\n\n// Resolve `unsafePath` against `baseDir` and assert the result stays inside\n// `baseDir`. Defends against zip entries like `../../etc/passwd` and absolute\n// paths slipped past `join()`.\nexport function resolveInside(baseDir: string, unsafePath: string): string {\n  const resolved = resolve(baseDir, unsafePath);\n  if (resolved !== baseDir && !resolved.startsWith(baseDir + sep)) {\n    throw new Error(`Path escapes install root: ${unsafePath}`);\n  }\n  return resolved;\n}\n"
}
```

  "new_string": "import { existsSync, mkdirSync, readdirSync, rmSync, statSync } from \"fs\";\nimport { writeFile } from \"fs/promises\";\nimport { homedir } from \"os\";\nimport { dirname, join } from \"path\";\nimport { unzipSync } from \"fflate\";\n\nimport { assertValidSlug, resolveInside } from \"./safe-paths\";\n\nconst SKILLS_DIR = join(homedir(), \".claude\", \"skills\");\n\nexport async function installSkill(slug: string, zipBuffer: ArrayBuffer): Promise<string> {\n  assertValidSlug(slug);\n  const destDir = resolveInside(SKILLS_DIR, slug);\n  if (!existsSync(SKILLS_DIR)) mkdirSync(SKILLS_DIR, { recursive: true });\n  if (existsSync(destDir)) rmSync(destDir, { recursive: true, force: true });\n  mkdirSync(destDir, { recursive: true });\n\n  const files = unzipSync(new Uint8Array(zipBuffer));\n  try {\n    for (const [entryPath, content] of Object.entries(files)) {\n      if (entryPath.endsWith(\"/\")) continue; // skip directory entries\n      const dest = resolveInside(destDir, entryPath);\n      const parentDir = dirname(dest);\n      if (!existsSync(parentDir)) mkdirSync(parentDir, { recursive: true });\n      await writeFile(dest, content);\n    }\n  } catch (err) {\n    // Don't leave half-written skills on disk; the next install attempt will then\n    // start from a clean slate instead of merging onto a corrupt tree.\n    rmSync(destDir, { recursive: true, force: true });\n    throw err;\n  }\n\n  return destDir;\n}\n\nexport async function uninstallSkill(slug: string): Promise<boolean> {\n  assertValidSlug(slug);\n  const destDir = resolveInside(SKILLS_DIR, slug);\n  if (!existsSync(destDir)) return false;\n  rmSync(destDir, { recursive: true, force: true });\n  return true;\n}"

import { defineVitestConfig } from "@nuxt/test-utils/config"

export default defineVitestConfig({
  test: {
    environment: "happy-dom",
    globals: false,
    include: [
      "shared/**/*.test.ts",
      "server/**/*.test.ts",
      "app/**/*.test.ts",
      "tests/contract/**/*.test.ts",
    ],
    exclude: ["node_modules", ".nuxt", ".output", "tests/e2e/**"],
    coverage: {
      provider: "v8",
      reporter: ["text", "html", "lcov"],
      // Coverage targets the surfaces this suite *actually* exercises.
      // server/repositories/** is covered by the integration suite (see
      // vitest.config.integration.ts) — not measured here. Files like
      // server/api/**, server/utils/{auth,db,storage,inngest,publish}.ts,
      // server/utils/jobs/**, app/pages/**, app/components/{extension,
      // filters,publish,ui}/** are out of scope for this round and stay
      // outside the include list rather than being silently averaged in
      // at 0%.
      include: [
        "shared/validators/**",
        "shared/search/**",
        "shared/extensions/**",
        "shared/installs/**",
        "shared/approvals/**",
        "shared/tags.ts",
        "shared/taxonomy.ts",
        "shared/theme.ts",
        "app/composables/useFilters.ts",
        "app/composables/usePublishWizard.ts",
        "app/composables/useTheme.ts",
        "server/utils/extensions-state.ts",
        "server/utils/installs.ts",
        "server/utils/approvals.ts",
        "server/utils/auth.ts",
        "app/components/approvals/**",
        "app/components/profile/SectionRequests.vue",
      ],
      exclude: [
        "**/*.test.ts",
        "**/*.spec.ts",
        ".nuxt/**",
        ".output/**",
      ],
      thresholds: {
        // Global floor — set at the lowest currently-covered subdir.
        // Tighten as additional surfaces gain real coverage; each future
        // bump should land in its own commit so the ratchet is visible.
        lines: 85,
        branches: 75,
        functions: 85,
        statements: 85,
        // Pure logic + state machine decisions — load-bearing, easy to
        // cover. The 87.8 branches on shared/search reflects unexercised
        // filter chips (free/official); raise to 90 after a follow-up
        // test pass.
        "shared/validators/**":   { lines: 95, branches: 90, functions: 95, statements: 95 },
        "shared/search/**":       { lines: 95, branches: 87, functions: 95, statements: 95 },
        "shared/extensions/**":   { lines: 95, branches: 90, functions: 95, statements: 95 },
        "shared/installs/**":     { lines: 95, branches: 90, functions: 95, statements: 95 },
        "shared/approvals/**":    { lines: 95, branches: 90, functions: 95, statements: 95 },
        "shared/tags.ts":         { lines: 95, branches: 90, functions: 95, statements: 95 },
        "shared/taxonomy.ts":     { lines: 95, branches: 90, functions: 95, statements: 95 },
        "shared/theme.ts":        { lines: 95, branches: 90, functions: 95, statements: 95 },
        // Orchestrators with mocked-repo tests. The 87.5 branch on

        // short-circuit where one arm is exercised but the other path

        "server/utils/installs.ts":         { lines: 95, branches: 85, functions: 95, statements: 95 },

{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/cli/src/installers/safe-paths.test.ts",
  "content": "import { describe, expect, it } from \"vitest\";\nimport { join, sep } from \"path\";\n\nimport { assertValidSlug, resolveInside } from \"./safe-paths\";\n\ndescribe(\"assertValidSlug\", () => {\n  it(\"accepts a lowercase kebab-case slug\", () => {\n    expect(() => assertValidSlug(\"hello\")).not.toThrow();\n    expect(() => assertValidSlug(\"hello-world\")).not.toThrow();\n    expect(() => assertValidSlug(\"abc123\")).not.toThrow();\n  });\n\n  it(\"rejects path-traversal payloads\", () => {\n    expect(() => assertValidSlug(\"../etc/passwd\")).toThrow();\n    expect(() => assertValidSlug(\"..\")).toThrow();\n    expect(() => assertValidSlug(\"foo/bar\")).toThrow();\n  });\n\n  it(\"rejects absolute paths and other shell-significant inputs\", () => {\n    expect(() => assertValidSlug(\"/etc/passwd\")).toThrow();\n    expect(() => assertValidSlug(\"foo bar\")).toThrow();\n    expect(() => assertValidSlug(\"foo;rm\")).toThrow();\n    expect(() => assertValidSlug(\"Foo\")).toThrow(); // uppercase\n    expect(() => assertValidSlug(\"\")).toThrow();\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe(\"resolveInside\", () => {\n  const root = join(\"/\", \"tmp\", \"agentcenter-test-root\");\n\n  it(\"resolves a simple child path inside the root\", () => {\n    expect(resolveInside(root, \"hello.txt\")).toBe(join(root, \"hello.txt\"));\n  });\n\n  it(\"resolves nested paths inside the root\", () => {\n    expect(resolveInside(root, \"nested/dir/file.txt\")).toBe(\n      join(root, \"nested\", \"dir\", \"file.txt\"),\n    );\n  });\n\n  it(\"throws on traversal that escapes the root\", () => {\n    expect(() => resolveInside(root, \"../escape.txt\")).toThrow(/escapes/);\n    expect(() => resolveInside(root, \"nested/../../escape.txt\")).toThrow(/escapes/);\n  });\n\n  it(\"throws on absolute paths outside the root\", () => {\n    expect(() => resolveInside(root, \"/etc/passwd\")).toThrow(/escapes/);\n  });\n\n  it(\"allows entries that .. their way back inside the root\", () => {\n    // a/b/../c resolves to a/c — still inside.\n    expect(resolveInside(root, \"a/b/../c.txt\")).toBe(join(root, \"a\", \"c.txt\"));\n  });\n\n  it(\"does not match a sibling root that shares a prefix\", () => {\n    // baseDir = /tmp/agentcenter-test-root\n    // attacker entry = \"../agentcenter-test-rootEVIL/file.txt\"\n    // resolves to /tmp/agentcenter-test-rootEVIL/file.txt which starts with\n    // the same string prefix but NOT with `baseDir + sep` — the sep check\n    // is the guard.\n    expect(() =>\n      resolveInside(root, `..${sep}agentcenter-test-rootEVIL${sep}file.txt`),\n    ).toThrow(/escapes/);\n  });\n});\n"
}

{
  "command": "git commit -m \"$(cat <<'EOF'\nfix(cli-installers): validate slug and zip entry paths to prevent traversal\n\nA malicious zip with an entry like `../../etc/foo` would have escaped\nthe installer's intended root directory; same for a crafted slug. The\nnew safe-paths helper rejects invalid slugs at the entrypoint and\nresolves every zip entry inside the destination dir, throwing if the\nresolved path escapes. Both installers (skills and cli) route through\nthe same helper so the fix can't drift between them.\n\nPre-existing in skills.ts; the bug just became more visible after\ncli.ts copied the same shape. Flagged on PR #52 by CodeRabbit.\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -8",

}

```
1	# Deployment
2	
3	AgentCenter (Nuxt) ships as a standalone Node server, with Postgres + an object store + Inngest behind it. Three deploy targets are supported:
4	
5	- **Node** (default) — `nitro: { preset: "node-server" }`. Runs anywhere Node 22+ runs: bare metal, container, PaaS.
6	- **Vercel** (auto-detected) — Nitro picks `preset: "vercel"` when `process.env.VERCEL` is set. The `vercel-build` script in `package.json` chains `drizzle-kit migrate && bun scripts/seed-mcp-landscape.ts && bun scripts/seed-editorial-collections.ts && bun scripts/seed-catalog.ts && nuxt build`, so every deploy: (1) applies pending migrations against `DATABASE_URL`, then (2) idempotently upserts the static MCP panorama landscape, editorial collections, and catalog marketplace stubs, then (3) builds. Aborts the deploy if any step fails (prevents code shipping against a stale or empty schema). The destructive `db:seed` script (demo users + sample extensions) is **not** in the build path — that stays a one-shot manual operation.
7	- **Cloudflare** (stretch) — `nitro: { preset: "cloudflare_module" }`. Workers + R2 binding. Not maintained alongside primary; expect bundle-size + driver tweaks.
8	
9	This guide covers the Node path. Vercel needs only the `DATABASE_URL` env var set in the project (Build & Runtime). The Cloudflare path is a config flip plus a storage driver swap.
10	
11	## Prerequisites
12	
13	| Service | Purpose | Required |
14	|---|---|---|
15	| **PostgreSQL 15+** | Application database | yes |
16	| **Object storage** — Supabase Storage **or** Cloudflare R2 | Extension bundles | yes |
17	| **Inngest** account (cloud) or local dev server | Background jobs (scan, index) | yes (cloud for prod) |
18	| **Node 22+** | Runtime | yes |
19	| **Bun 1.3.12** | Build (production runs Node, not Bun) | yes during build |
20	
21	## 1. Provision the database
22	
23	Any Postgres works — Supabase, Neon, AWS RDS, self-hosted. Apply migrations:
24	
25	```bash
26	bun install
27	DATABASE_URL="postgresql://..." bun run db:migrate
28	DATABASE_URL="postgresql://..." bun run db:apply-fts
29	DATABASE_URL="postgresql://..." bun run db:seed   # optional: chains seed.ts + seed-catalog.ts (16 demo + 66 catalog)
30	```
31	
32	The `db:apply-fts` step requires `psql` on PATH. It runs `drizzle/0002_fts_search_vector.sql` against your DB (FTS column + trgm indexes).
33	
34	### Seeding super-admins and reviewer matrix
35	
36	The approval workflow needs at least one super-admin so the
37	`/admin/reviewers` matrix is reachable. `bun run db:seed` promotes
38	one of the seeded `CREATORS` users to `role = 'superAdmin'`:
39	
40	```bash
41	SEED_SUPER_ADMIN_EMAIL="amy@agentcenter.dev" bun run db:seed
42	```
43	
44	`SEED_SUPER_ADMIN_EMAIL` defaults to `amy@agentcenter.dev` (the first
45	creator in `scripts/seed.ts`). Supply any email from `CREATORS` to
46	pick a different super-admin; an unknown email logs a warning and
47	seeds no super-admin (the matrix UI then 403s for every user — fix
48	by re-running with a valid email).
49	
50	The same seed populates the admin matrix from
51	`shared/data/approval-admins.ts` so every demo extension type tab
52	has covering admins (All / macro / micro across Company and the
53	product lines) on a fresh DB.
54	
55	`db:seed` is destructive (TRUNCATE … CASCADE on orgs and tags) and
56	is the "reset to demo state" command. Production deploys use only
57	the idempotent seeds in the `vercel-build` chain.
58	
59	To prevent typos from wiping real data, `db:seed` refuses to run
60	against a non-localhost `DATABASE_URL` unless `SEED_ALLOW_REMOTE=1`
61	is set. See the staging workflow below; **do not set this flag
62	against a production environment**.
63	
64	### Seeding a staging Vercel environment for demos
65	
66	If your Vercel deployment is a staging / demo environment (separate
67	project or separate database from real prod), you can plant the full
68	demo dataset there so sign-in works and the admin surfaces are
69	populated.
70	
71	1. **Link the local repo** to the Vercel project (one-time):
72	
73	   ```bash
74	   vercel link
75	   ```
76	
77	2. **Pull the env for the right environment** into a local file:
78	
79	   ```bash
80	   vercel env pull .env.staging --environment=<env>
81	   ```
82	
83	   Pick `<env>` based on your project's shape:
84	
85	   - **Single-environment project** (the only deployed URL is the
86	     Vercel `production` env, no real prod users yet) → use
87	     `--environment=production`. The demo site IS the production
88	     environment; that's where the seed needs to land.
89	   - **Separate staging Vercel project** (e.g. `agentcenter-staging`
90	     distinct from `agentcenter-prod`) → use `--environment=production`
91	     against the staging project. Same logic — its `production` is
92	     your demo.
93	   - **Single project hosting both real prod and a preview branch**
94	     → use `--environment=preview`. `production` here means the live
95	     site with real users; do not seed it.
96	
97	   When in doubt, run `vercel env ls` to list environments and confirm
98	   what's there before pulling. The `SEED_ALLOW_REMOTE=1` guardrail in
99	   step 5 is a checkpoint, not a substitute for knowing which DB the
100	   pulled `DATABASE_URL` actually points at.
101	
102	3. **Export into the shell**:
103	
104	   ```bash
105	   set -a; source .env.staging; set +a
106	   ```
107	
108	4. **Confirm the target host** before the destructive step:
109	
110	   ```bash
111	   echo "$DATABASE_URL" | grep -oE '@[^/]+'
112	   ```
113	
114	   Read the hostname aloud. If it looks like prod, stop.
115	
116	5. **Run the seed**:
117	
118	   ```bash
119	   SEED_PASSWORD=demo SEED_ALLOW_REMOTE=1 bun run db:seed
120	   ```
121	
122	   `SEED_ALLOW_REMOTE=1` is the explicit acknowledgement that you
123	   know the URL points somewhere remote. Without it, the script
124	   bails before connecting.
125	
126	6. **Sign in** at `https://<your-staging-host>/sign-in` as
127	   `amy@agentcenter.dev` with the password from step 5. The
128	   UserButton dropdown will surface "Approval queue" and "Reviewer
129	   matrix" entries (super-admin only).
130	
131	7. **Rotate the demo password** any time by re-running step 5 with
132	   a different `SEED_PASSWORD`. The seed is idempotent — re-runs
133	   reset the DB to a known demo state and rotate all 6 demo users'
134	   passwords in one shot.
135	
136	8. **Don't commit `.env.staging`** — `.gitignore` already covers
137	   `*.env*`, but `git status` after step 2 is a worthwhile habit.
138	
139	`vercel-build` runs on every subsequent deploy and only touches the
140	idempotent seeds (mcp-landscape, editorial-collections, catalog) —
141	the demo creators, extensions, and approval-reviewer matrix planted
142	by step 5 survive across deploys.
143	
144	## 2. Provision object storage
145	
146	### Supabase Storage (default)
147	
148	1. Supabase dashboard → Storage → New bucket (e.g. `agentcenter-bundles`)
149	2. Generate a service-role key (Settings → API → service_role secret)
150	3. Set env: `SUPABASE_URL`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, `SUPABASE_BUCKET`
151	
152	### Cloudflare R2 (alternative)
153	
154	1. Cloudflare dashboard → R2 → Create bucket
155	2. Create an API token with Object Read & Write on the bucket
156	3. Set env: `R2_ACCOUNT_ID`, `R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID`, `R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY`, `R2_BUCKET`
157	4. Apply CORS so browsers can `PUT` direct to R2 (the upload-sign endpoint hands out presigned URLs; the browser uploads without proxying through the app)
158	
159	`R2_ACCOUNT_ID` being set picks R2 over Supabase automatically. See `server/utils/storage.ts`.
160	
161	## 3. Inngest
162	
163	1. Create an Inngest app (cloud) and copy `INNGEST_EVENT_KEY` + `INNGEST_SIGNING_KEY`
164	2. After deploying, register the webhook URL in the Inngest dashboard:
165	   ```text
166	   https://yourdomain.com/api/inngest
167	   ```
168	3. Inngest will verify by calling the endpoint; `scan-bundle` and `reindex-search` will appear as registered functions
169	
170	For local dev, run the Inngest dev server instead:
171	```bash
172	bunx inngest-cli@1.19.2 dev
173	```
174	It auto-discovers `/api/inngest` and routes events locally. No event/signing keys needed locally.
175	
176	## 4. Build & run
177	
178	```bash
179	bun install --frozen-lockfile
180	bun run build
181	PORT=3000 node .output/server/index.mjs
182	```
183	
184	Front the Node process with a reverse proxy (nginx, Caddy) for TLS, gzip, and HTTP/2.
185	
186	### Required env vars at runtime
187	
188	| Variable | Purpose |
189	|---|---|
190	| `DATABASE_URL` | Postgres connection string |
191	| `BETTER_AUTH_URL` | Public origin (e.g. `https://agentcenter.example.com`) |
192	| `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` | Random 32+ char string (`openssl rand -base64 32`) |
193	| `NUXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL` | Public origin (same as `BETTER_AUTH_URL`) |
194	| `NODE_ENV=production` | Enables prod cookies, prod Inngest routing, etc. |
195	| `INNGEST_EVENT_KEY` | Inngest cloud event key |
196	| `INNGEST_SIGNING_KEY` | Inngest cloud webhook verification key |
197	| `SUPABASE_URL` + `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` + `SUPABASE_BUCKET` | Storage (if using Supabase) |
198	| `R2_ACCOUNT_ID` + `R2_ACCESS_KEY_ID` + `R2_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY` + `R2_BUCKET` | Storage (if using R2) |
199	
200	See `.env.example` for the canonical list.
201	
202	## 5. Health checks
203	
204	After the server is running:
205	
206	- `curl https://yourdomain.com/api/v1/extensions` → 200 with `{ items, total, page, pageSize }`
207	- `curl -I https://yourdomain.com/` → 302 to `/en`
208	- `curl https://yourdomain.com/en/extensions` → 200 with the browse page HTML
209	- Sign-up via the UI → land on `/en/onboard` → pick a department → land on `/en/`
210	- Publish a test bundle (zip with valid `manifest.toml`) via `/en/publish/new` → scope=personal → Inngest scans → row appears on `/en/extensions`
211	- Build the CLI binary from `../agentcenter/cli/`, point at production URL, run `agentcenter login` end-to-end through the device-code flow
212	
213	## 6. Upgrades
214	
215	Migrations are idempotent (Drizzle journal-based). For zero-downtime upgrades:
216	
217	1. Apply new migrations against the live DB before swapping app binaries
218	2. Run the FTS migration once (only re-run if `drizzle/0002_fts_search_vector.sql` was changed)
219	3. Hot-swap the Node process behind the reverse proxy (PM2, systemd, blue/green)
220	
221	## 7. Cloudflare deploy (stretch)
222	
223	```ts
224	// nuxt.config.ts
225	nitro: { preset: "cloudflare_module" }
226	```
227	
228	Plus:
229	- Swap `drizzle-orm/postgres-js` (TCP) for `@neondatabase/serverless` (HTTP) or Hyperdrive — Workers can't open raw TCP
230	- Use the R2 binding (`event.context.cloudflare.env.BUCKET`) instead of the S3 SDK; cuts bundle size significantly
231	- `wrangler secret put` for each runtime env var
232	
233	Both swaps are localized to `server/utils/db.ts` and `server/utils/storage.ts`. Everything else is preset-agnostic.
234	
235	This path is documented but not maintained alongside the Node path. If you need it, expect a half-day of bundle-size triage on first deploy.
236	
```

---
// Idempotent catalog seed.
//
// Safe to run on every deploy. Mirrors scripts/seed-mcp-landscape.ts in
// strategy: upserts a fixed inventory of extensions (id prefix `cat-`) plus
// the small set of supporting rows it needs (default org, tag vocabulary),
// then prunes any catalog-prefixed rows no longer in the inventory. Real
// user-published extensions (any non-`cat-` id) are never touched.
//
// Wired into the Vercel build via `vercel-build` so a freshly deployed
// environment carries a populated /extensions catalog.

import { and, inArray, notInArray, sql } from "drizzle-orm"
import { drizzle, type PostgresJsDatabase } from "drizzle-orm/postgres-js"
import postgres from "postgres"

import { CATALOG, type CatalogEntry } from "../shared/data/catalog"
import * as schema from "../shared/db/schema"
import {
  extensions,
  extensionTags,
  organizations,
  tags,
} from "../shared/db/schema"
import { TAG_LABELS } from "../shared/tags"

type Db = PostgresJsDatabase<typeof schema>

const SYSTEM_ORG_ID = "default"
const CATALOG_ID_PREFIX = "cat-"

function extensionIdFor(entry: CatalogEntry): string {
  return `${CATALOG_ID_PREFIX}${entry.slug}`
}

/**
 * Upserts the catalog into `extensions` + `extension_tags`. Re-runnable;
 * never destructive to user data. The whole body runs inside one
 * transaction so a mid-step failure rolls back cleanly — particularly
 * important for step 4, where a crash between the delete and the insert
 * would otherwise leave catalog extensions with no tag links.
 *
 *   1. Ensure SYSTEM_ORG_ID exists (the owner for catalog rows).
 *   2. Upsert every tag from TAG_LABELS so referenced ids resolve in prod.
 *   3. Upsert each catalog entry by extension id.
 *   4. Replace tag links for each upserted extension (delete-then-insert
 *      scoped by extensionId — surgical, only the catalog row is touched).
 *   5. Prune extensions with id LIKE 'cat-%' not in the current CATALOG.
 */
export async function seedCatalog(db: Db): Promise<void> {
  await db.transaction(async (tx) => {
    // 1. Owner org — same constant as seed-mcp-landscape; safe to upsert again.
    await tx
      .insert(organizations)
      .values({
        id: SYSTEM_ORG_ID,
        slug: SYSTEM_ORG_ID,
        name: "Default Organization",
        nameZh: "默认组织",
      })
      .onConflictDoNothing({ target: organizations.id })

    // 2. Tag vocabulary. seed.ts seeds these in dev; in prod nothing else
    //    does, so we upsert here too. onConflictDoNothing keeps existing
    //    rows verbatim in case admin curation has edited a label by hand.
    const tagRows = Object.entries(TAG_LABELS).map(([id, label]) => ({
      id,
      labelEn: label.en,
      labelZh: label.zh,
    }))
    await tx.insert(tags).values(tagRows).onConflictDoNothing()
    console.log(`seed-catalog: ensured ${tagRows.length} tags`)

    // 3. Extension upserts. Catalog rows are visibility=published with a
    //    fixed publishedAt (so re-runs don't shift sort order); ownerOrgId
    //    is the SYSTEM_ORG; publisherUserId is null (the schema allows it).
    //    deptId stays null — catalog rows are department-agnostic.
    const now = new Date()
    const extRows = CATALOG.map((entry) => ({
      id: extensionIdFor(entry),
      slug: entry.slug,

// Catalog data for the idempotent seed. Each entry becomes one row in the
// `extensions` table with id = `cat-${slug}`. `scripts/seed-catalog.ts` upserts
// these on every deploy via the `vercel-build` chain, so editing this file
// changes prod content on the next deploy.
//
// Slugs must NOT collide with the destructive demo seed (`scripts/seed.ts`),
// which uses `web-search-pro`, `code-interpreter`, `image-vision`,
// `arch-reviewer`, `data-analyst`, `test-generator`, `github-mcp`,
// `postgres-mcp`, `slack-mcp`, `k8s-mcp`, `summarize`, `translate`, `explain`,
// `notion-sync`, `calendar-agent`, `mqtt-bridge`. Slugs here are distinct so
// both seeds can coexist in dev without unique-constraint conflicts.

import type { IconColor } from "./icon-colors"

interface CatalogEntryBase {
  slug: string
  category: "skills" | "slash" | "plugins"
  scope: "personal" | "org" | "enterprise"
  funcCat: "workTask" | "business" | "tools"
  subCat: string
  l2: string
  name: string
  nameZh: string
  tagline: string
  taglineZh: string
  description: string
  descriptionZh: string
  iconEmoji: string
  iconColor: IconColor
  tags: string[]
  badge?: "official" | "popular" | "new"
  downloadsCount: number
  starsAvg: string
}

// Discriminated union: a productLine-tier catalog row must declare its
// product line at the type level. Without this, an entry that ships
// `officialTier: "productLine"` without a line would silently land in
// the DB as Wireless on the next deploy (because the catalog seed runs
// on every build); the union makes that mistake a typecheck failure.
type CatalogEntryUnofficial = CatalogEntryBase & {
  officialTier?: undefined
  productLineId?: undefined
}
type CatalogEntryCompany = CatalogEntryBase & {
  officialTier: "company"
  productLineId?: undefined
}
type CatalogEntryProductLine = CatalogEntryBase & {
  officialTier: "productLine"

3:Marketplace for AI agent extensions (Skills, MCP servers, slash commands, plugins) with internal-enterprise features (departments, scope, tags, collections) and a companion CLI for actual machine-side install. Bilingual (EN/ZH) from day one.
39:12. **Official-tier approval workflow**: extensions default to Unofficial; elevation to Product-Line Official or Company Official goes through an admin matrix indexed by 5 coords — `(extensionCategory × tier × productLineId? × categoryLevel × categoryKey)`. `extensionCategory` reuses the existing `extension_category` enum so the matrix is tabbed per ext-type; `categoryLevel` is `all | macro | micro` (wildcard / FUNC_TAXONOMY l1 / l2); `productLineId` participates only for the `productLine` tier. Tiers are independent (no PL→Company ladder), `officialTier` lives in its own enum column, and the `/api/v1` `badge` field is read-derived from it to preserve the frozen CLI contract. Matrix edits use a 2-D cover relation: super-admins everywhere; any user whose admin row covers the target cell on both the column-tier axis (`(company, null) ⊇ (productLine, X)`) and the category axis (`all ⊇ macro ⊇ micro`) within the same extensionCategory. Routing is fan-out on the category dim and EXACT on column-tier (company admins do NOT receive product-line requests for review). Revocation is a super-admin action from the detail page; revocation metadata (timestamp / admin / required note) lives on `extensions` and clears on the next approval. Full rationale and trade-offs in `docs/adr/0001-official-tier-approval-workflow.md` (including the 2026-06-08, 2026-06-09, and 2026-06-09b updates).
---PLAN---
3:Marketplace for AI agent extensions (Skills, MCP servers, slash commands, plugins) with internal-enterprise features (departments, scope, tags, collections) and a companion CLI for actual machine-side install.
128:│   └── plugins/
197:| `/[locale]` | Home — featured banner + Trending Skills + Browse All grid | **P0** |
524:- CLI installers for MCP / slash commands / plugins (in the carried-over CLI repo)
537:`bun run validate` is the primary command — the fast inner loop that mirrors the first four steps of CI. It chains four steps in order, failing fast. The fifth gate, `bun run test:integration`, runs PGlite-backed repository tests; CI runs it on every PR but it's not part of `validate` (kept separate so the local typecheck/test loop stays under a few seconds). Phase 0 landed a working `bun run validate` against the bare scaffold; every subsequent phase keeps it green.
686:server/utils/, server/api/, server/plugins/, server/jobs/
701:  ignores: ["server/repositories/**", "server/utils/db.ts", "server/plugins/db.ts"],
710:  files: ["server/repositories/**", "server/utils/db.ts", "server/plugins/db.ts"],
724:The `server/**` rule with `ignores` ensures Drizzle imports outside `server/repositories/`, `server/utils/db.ts`, and `server/plugins/db.ts` are rejected at lint time. This is what makes the repository boundary load-bearing rather than a convention.
---README---
3:A bilingual (EN/ZH) marketplace for AI agent extensions — Skills, MCP servers, slash commands, and plugins. Nuxt 4 rewrite of the original Next.js implementation; same `/api/v1` contract so the existing CLI binary keeps working.
---API---
76:| `category` | `skills` \| `mcp` \| `slash` \| `plugins` | Filter by extension category |
78:| `funcCat` | `workTask` \| `business` \| `tools` | Filter by functional category |
79:| `subCat` | string | Filter by L1 subcategory |
95:      "category": "skills",
125:  "category": "skills",
---FEATURES---
11:**Overview.** A bilingual marketplace for AI agent extensions (Skills, MCP servers, slash commands, plugins). Users can browse and search a catalog with rich filters, view detail pages with READMEs and stats, publish their own extensions through a 4-step wizard with automated bundle scanning, save favorites, and authorize a CLI for installs. Open sign-up, EN/ZH UI and content, themes, and a public registry API. Multi-tenant schema with a single-tenant UI in v1.
15:- **Type-first browsing** — Browsing the marketplace starts by picking a type — Skills, MCP Servers, Slash Commands, or Plugins — from the Explore dropdown in the top bar. The unfiltered catalog stays reachable but isn't the default destination.
16:- **Function-types sidebar** — A persistent sidebar tree exposes the three-level functional taxonomy (Work Task / Business / Tools, plus sub-categories and leaves). Selecting any node filters the catalog and survives URL sharing.
28:- **Home: trending row** — A cross-category mix of trending extensions (Skills, MCP servers, slash commands, plugins).
41:### MCP Panorama
44:- **A software tool can ship multiple MCPs** — A tool may expose any number of MCP servers (CodeCheck ships two; K8sOps and ObservHub each ship three). Each MCP carries its own status, dependent count, and marketplace link.
46:- **Tool-as-card layout** — Each software tool reads as a small quiet card with a serif name; the MCP servers inside render as vivid status-coloured pills. PDTs sit as subtly recessed regions for clear visual hierarchy.
48:- **Status filter chips** — Show only released, only in-development, or only no-MCP-needed MCPs.
52:- **MCP detail panel** — Click any pill to see the MCP's status, dependent count, owning team, endpoint, and tags. The panel also lists sibling MCPs of the same tool, so you can switch between them in one click. Released MCPs offer a "Copy MCP URI" button and a direct link to the marketplace listing; in-development MCPs offer "Track progress"; tools without an MCP offer "Request MCP build".
53:- **Bilingual** — Sectors, domains, PDTs, statuses, tool names, and MCP descriptions all render in EN or ZH.
```

1	# AgentCenter Feature Inventory · 功能清单
2	
3	Source-of-truth list of what's shipped today, organized by capability area. For what changed when, see [daily-log.md](./daily-log.md).
4	
5	当前已交付的能力清单，按功能领域分组。每日变更记录见 [daily-log.md](./daily-log.md)。
6	
7	---
8	
9	## English
10	
11	**Overview.** A bilingual marketplace for AI agent extensions (Skills, MCP servers, slash commands, plugins). Users can browse and search a catalog with rich filters, view detail pages with READMEs and stats, publish their own extensions through a 4-step wizard with automated bundle scanning, save favorites, and authorize a CLI for installs. Open sign-up, EN/ZH UI and content, themes, and a public registry API. Multi-tenant schema with a single-tenant UI in v1.
12	
13	### Browse & discover
14	
15	- **Type-first browsing** — Browsing the marketplace starts by picking a type — Skills, MCP Servers, Slash Commands, or Plugins — from the Explore dropdown in the top bar. The unfiltered catalog stays reachable but isn't the default destination.
16	- **Function-types sidebar** — A persistent sidebar tree exposes the three-level functional taxonomy (Work Task / Business / Tools, plus sub-categories and leaves). Selecting any node filters the catalog and survives URL sharing.
17	- **Extension catalog** — Browse all published extensions in a paginated grid at `/extensions`.
18	- **Search** — Search by any phrase, in English or Chinese, including short fragments.
19	- **Department picker** — Narrow to a department and all of its sub-departments.
20	- **Scope filter** — Filter by Personal, Organization, or Enterprise.
21	- **Tag filters** — Multi-select tags with "any" or "all" matching.
22	- **Sort** — Sort by downloads, stars, or most recently updated.
23	- **Creator filter** — Filter by the user who published the extension.
24	- **Publisher filter** — Filter by the organization behind the extension.
25	- **Shareable filter state** — Every filter combination is reflected in the URL, so any search can be bookmarked or shared.
26	- **Active-filter chips** — Above the results grid, each applied filter (search term, scope, department, creator, publisher, every selected tag) renders as a removable chip — click × to drop one, or "Clear all" to clear them in a single tap.
27	- **Home: featured spotlight** — A curated "Featured this week" extension anchors the home page with its name, tagline, install command, and a deep link to the detail page; falls back to a slim editorial card when no extension is marked featured.
28	- **Home: trending row** — A cross-category mix of trending extensions (Skills, MCP servers, slash commands, plugins).
29	- **Whole-card click** — Click anywhere on an extension card to open it; Save / Install stay independently clickable.
30	
31	### Detail page
32	
33	- **README** — The extension's README rendered with safe markdown.
34	- **Sidebar metadata** — Homepage, repo, license, scope, last updated.
35	- **Stats** — Average rating, downloads, version.
36	- **Install command** — A one-click copyable CLI snippet on the Setup tab.
37	- **Tabs** — Overview, Setup, Permissions. Permissions surfaces the network / files / runtime / data toggles the publisher declared.
38	- **Related extensions** — A sidebar list of suggestions related to the current extension, each carrying its badge, owning department, and star rating beside the name.
39	- **Share** — A canonical link that's safe to share across hosts; uses the native share sheet on iOS / Android with a clipboard fallback elsewhere.
40

11:**Overview.** A bilingual marketplace for AI agent extensions (Skills, MCP servers, slash commands, plugins). Users can browse and search a catalog with rich filters, view detail pages with READMEs and stats, publish their own extensions through a 4-step wizard with automated bundle scanning, save favorites, and authorize a CLI for installs. Open sign-up, EN/ZH UI and content, themes, and a public registry API. Multi-tenant schema with a single-tenant UI in v1.
15:- **Type-first browsing** — Browsing the marketplace starts by picking a type — Skills, MCP Servers, Slash Commands, or Plugins — from the Explore dropdown in the top bar. The unfiltered catalog stays reachable but isn't the default destination.
28:- **Home: trending row** — A cross-category mix of trending extensions (Skills, MCP servers, slash commands, plugins).
41:### MCP Panorama
44:- **A software tool can ship multiple MCPs** — A tool may expose any number of MCP servers (CodeCheck ships two; K8sOps and ObservHub each ship three). Each MCP carries its own status, dependent count, and marketplace link.
46:- **Tool-as-card layout** — Each software tool reads as a small quiet card with a serif name; the MCP servers inside render as vivid status-coloured pills. PDTs sit as subtly recessed regions for clear visual hierarchy.
48:- **Status filter chips** — Show only released, only in-development, or only no-MCP-needed MCPs.
52:- **MCP detail panel** — Click any pill to see the MCP's status, dependent count, owning team, endpoint, and tags. The panel also lists sibling MCPs of the same tool, so you can switch between them in one click. Released MCPs offer a "Copy MCP URI" button and a direct link to the marketplace listing; in-development MCPs offer "Track progress"; tools without an MCP offer "Request MCP build".
53:- **Bilingual** — Sectors, domains, PDTs, statuses, tool names, and MCP descriptions all render in EN or ZH.
54:- **Catalog cross-link** — Filtering the extensions catalog to MCP Servers surfaces a "View Panorama" banner above the grid; MCP Panorama also lives inside the Explore dropdown in the top nav, next to the type filters.
71:- **Apply-for-official dialog** — From the publisher dashboard, an extension that's published and unofficial gets an "Apply for official" action: pick a tier, pick a functional category (defaults to the extension's existing subCat), pick a product line when the tier is Product-Line, optionally explain why.
73:- **Reviewer queue** — Each (tier × functional category × productLine?) cell is owned by an assigned set of reviewers. `/admin/approvals` shows the reviewer their queue. Approve in one click; reject opens an inline note field. The queue carries a small pill rail (Official + Category pickers) so reviewers can narrow a multi-cell assignment to one tier, one subCat, or one product line at a time; the URL is shareable.
124:- **Installs Skills today** — MCP / slash command / plugin installers are on the roadmap.
130:**概览。** 面向 AI Agent 扩展（Skill、MCP 服务器、斜杠命令、插件）的双语应用市场。用户可在带筛选的目录中浏览搜索、在详情页查看 README 与统计、通过 4 步向导发布自己的扩展（含自动扫描）、收藏喜欢的扩展，并授权 CLI 完成安装。开放注册、中英双语 UI 与内容、主题切换，以及对外的公共注册表 API。多租户 Schema，v1 UI 为单租户。
134:- **先选类型的浏览方式** ——浏览市场始终从选定类型开始（Skill、MCP 服务器、斜杠命令、插件），通过顶部导航的 Explore 下拉菜单进入。未筛选的全量列表仍可访问，但不再是默认入口。
147:- **首页：热门栏** ——跨类别混合展示热门扩展（Skill、MCP、斜杠命令、插件）。
160:### MCP 全景图
163:- **一个软件工具可承载多个 MCP** ——一个工具可以承载任意数量的 MCP 服务器（CodeCheck 承载两个；K8sOps 与 ObservHub 各自承载三个）。每个 MCP 都拥有独立的状态、依赖方数量与市场链接。
165:- **工具卡 + MCP 胶囊的排版** ——每个软件工具以一张安静的小卡片呈现，标题用衬线字；其下属的 MCP 服务器在卡内以醒目的状态色胶囊呈现。PDT 退化为微微凹陷的容器，版面层次清晰。
167:- **状态筛选** ——可单独查看"已发布"、"开发中"或"无需 MCP"的 MCP。
171:- **MCP 详情面板** ——点击任意胶囊即可查看该 MCP 的状态说明、依赖方数量、所属团队、endpoint 与标签。面板同时列出同一工具下的其他 MCP，可一键切换。已发布 MCP 提供"复制 MCP URI"按钮与直达市场详情的链接；开发中的 MCP 提供"跟踪进度"；尚无 MCP 的工具提供"申请 MCP 化"。
172:- **双语呈现** ——行业、领域、PDT、状态、工具名与 MCP 描述均支持中英双语。
173:- **目录交叉入口** ——在扩展目录筛选"MCP"分类时，列表上方会出现"查看全景图"横幅；MCP 全景图同时位于顶部导航的 Explore 下拉菜单中，与类型筛选并列。
243:- **当前支持 Skill 安装** —— MCP / 斜杠命令 / 插件的安装器在路线图上。

120	
121	- **Agent-agnostic** — Designed to work with multiple agents; Claude is the default target.
122	- **Distribution** — Ships as a Node JS bundle; cross-platform npm install ready.
123	- **Reads** — Pulls from the public registry API; no auth required for installs.

127	
128	## 中文
129	
130	**概览。** 面向 AI Agent 扩展（Skill、MCP 服务器、斜杠命令、插件）的双语应用市场。用户可在带筛选的目录中浏览搜索、在详情页查看 README 与统计、通过 4 步向导发布自己的扩展（含自动扫描）、收藏喜欢的扩展，并授权 CLI 完成安装。开放注册、中英双语 UI 与内容、主题切换，以及对外的公共注册表 API。多租户 Schema，v1 UI 为单租户。
131	
132	### 浏览与发现
133	
134	- **先选类型的浏览方式** ——浏览市场始终从选定类型开始（Skill、MCP 服务器、斜杠命令、插件），通过顶部导航的 Explore 下拉菜单进入。未筛选的全量列表仍可访问，但不再是默认入口。
135	- **侧边栏功能分类树** ——侧边栏常驻一棵三层功能分类树（工作任务 / 业务 / 工具，以及其下的子类和叶子节点）。选中任意节点即对目录进行筛选，选择反映在 URL 中，便于分享。
136	- **扩展目录** ——在 `/extensions` 浏览所有已发布扩展，支持分页。
137	- **搜索** ——任意短语搜索，支持中英文，含短片段查询。
138	- **部门选择器** ——收窄到某个部门及其所有子部门。
139	- **范围筛选** ——按"个人 / 组织 / 企业"筛选。
140	- **标签筛选** ——多选标签，支持"任意 / 全部"匹配。
141	- **排序** ——按下载量、评分或最近更新排序。
142	- **作者筛选** ——按发布扩展的用户筛选。
143	- **发布商筛选** ——按背后的组织筛选。
144	- **可分享的筛选状态** ——所有筛选条件都反映在 URL 中，可收藏或分享任意搜索。
145	- **已生效筛选标签** ——结果网格上方为每个生效的筛选（搜索词、范围、部门、作者、发布商、每个选中标签）显示一个可移除的标签——点击 × 移除单项，或点击"Clear all"一次全清。
146	- **首页：精选模块** ——首页顶部由一个真实的"本周精选"扩展担纲，展示其名称、宣传语、安装命令以及直达详情页的链接；若当前未标记任何精选，则回退到一张简洁的编辑卡片。
147	- **首页：热门栏** ——跨类别混合展示热门扩展（Skill、MCP、斜杠命令、插件）。
148	- **整卡可点击** ——点击扩展卡片任意位置都能打开；Save / Install 仍可独立点击。
149	
150	### 详情页
151	
152	- **README** ——以安全 Markdown 渲染扩展的 README。
153	- **侧边栏元数据** ——主页、代码仓、许可证、范围、最近更新时间。
154	- **统计** ——平均评分、下载量、版本。
155	- **安装命令** —— Setup 页签里提供一键可复制的 CLI 命令片段。
156	- **Tabs** ——概览 / 安装 / 权限。权限页签展示发布者声明的网络 / 文件 / 运行时 / 数据访问开关。
157	- **相关扩展** ——详情页侧栏列出与当前扩展相关的推荐，每条名称旁带徽章、所属部门与星级。
158	- **分享** ——跨域名安全分享的规范链接；iOS / Android 调用系统分享面板，其他平台回退到剪贴板复制。
159	
160	### MCP 全景图
161	
162	- **全景页** ——单页 `/mcp-panorama` 将所有内部工具服务以小卡片形式整屏呈现，按行业切片或公共服务领域 → PDT 分组归类。
163	- **一个软件工具可承载多个 MCP** ——一个工具可以承载任意数量的 MCP 服务器（CodeCheck 承载两个；K8sOps 与 ObservHub 各自承载三个）。每个 MCP 都拥有独立的状态、依赖方数量与市场链接。
164	- **层级切换** ——可在"行业服务"（12 个行业切片）与"公共服务"（5 个公共领域及其下属 PDT）之间切换。
165	- **工具卡 + MCP 胶囊的排版** ——每个软件工具以一张安静的小卡片呈现，标题用衬线字；其下属的 MCP 服务器在卡内以醒目的状态色胶囊呈现。PDT 退化为微微凹陷的容器，版面层次清晰。
166	- **覆盖率一目了然** ——每个分组的标题区展示总数与"已发布/总数"分数，配一条细分状态条；摘要区右栏按发布率列出排名前三的分组。
167	- **状态筛选** ——可单独查看"已发布"、"开发中"或"无需 MCP"的 MCP。
168	- **数字均可点击** ——页面上的每一个计数都是一个操作入口：点击分组标题下钻；点击状态总数翻转页面筛选（再次点击则恢复）；点击排名前三的任意条目即可直跳该分组。
169	- **下钻到行业或 PDT** ——点击层级侧栏任意一行、任意分组标题或排名前三任意一条，均可将视图收窄到该分组。
170	- **两种排版** ——紧凑的"全景图"视图，以及按"已发布 / 开发中 / 无需"三列拆分的"分组列表"备用视图。
171	- **MCP 详情面板** ——点击任意胶囊即可查看该 MCP 的状态说明、依赖方数量、所属团队、endpoint 与标签。面板同时列出同一工具下的其他 MCP，可一键切换。已发布 MCP 提供"复制 MCP URI"按钮与直达市场详情的链接；开发中的 MCP 提供"跟踪进度"；尚无 MCP 的工具提供"申请 MCP 化"。
172	- **双语呈现** ——行业、领域、PDT、状态、工具名与 MCP 描述均支持中英双语。
173	- **目录交叉入口** ——在扩展目录筛选"MCP"分类时，列表上方会出现"查看全景图"横幅；MCP 全景图同时位于顶部导航的 Explore 下拉菜单中，与类型筛选并列。
174	
175	### 发布
176	
177	- **4 步向导** ——基础信息 → 扩展包 → 上架信息 → 确认提交。
178	- **实时预览** ——固定面板镜像列表卡片与派生的 manifest，随表单填写而更新。
179	- **扩展包上传** ——从浏览器直接将扩展包上传到云存储。
```

```
# ADR-0001: Official-tier approval workflow

## Status

Accepted — 2026-06-07 (shipped via PR #41 merged as `a392c16`).

## Context

The product needed a moderation path for elevating a published extension
to "Official" status. The shape of the workflow was open along four
axes, each with plausible alternatives:

1. **What does "category" mean for routing?** The taxonomy already
   distinguished `funcCat` (3 top-level enum), `subCat` (text drawn
   from `FUNC_TAXONOMY.l1`, 9 leaves), and `deptId` (dotted-path text
   PK with descendant matching). Any one could plausibly drive the
   reviewer matrix.
2. **Are the two official tiers (Product-Line and Company) ordered?**
   A hierarchical ladder (PL first, then Company) reads naturally as
   "earn trust then escalate." Independent tiers let a publisher
   apply directly for Company without a PL gate.
3. **How does the existing `extensions.badge` column (already
   carrying `"official" | "popular" | "new"` and surfaced through the
   frozen `/api/v1` contract) interact with the new tier?** Reusing
   `badge` keeps the schema small; introducing a new column keeps the
   semantics clean.
4. **Who can edit the (tier × category) reviewer matrix?** Any
   `role='admin'` membership is the lightest model; a separate
   super-admin role is a smaller trust circle.

The CLI's `/api/v1` shape is frozen (see `docs/plan.md` §13 and
`docs/api.md`), so any choice that changed the public response was a
contract break and a much larger lift than the workflow needed.

## Decision

Four locked decisions, taken together:

1. **Category = `subCat`** (the FUNC_TAXONOMY l1 leaf). The matrix is
   `9 subCats × 2 tiers = 18` cells — concrete enough to assign
   without sprawl, fine-grained enough that a network specialist
   isn't asked to review documentation tooling.

2. **Tiers are independent, not hierarchical.** A publisher picks
   their target tier at submission time; Company supersedes
   Product-Line if both end up granted on the same extension. The
   orchestrator does not require a prior Product-Line approval before
   a Company request.

3. **`officialTier` is its own enum column.** `extensions.badge`
   stays in the DB unchanged. The `/api/v1/extensions/*` mappers
   *derive* `badge: "official"` from `officialTier != null` at the
   response boundary, so the frozen contract is preserved without a
   v2 bump and the CLI sees no change. App code stops writing `badge`
   entirely; future removal can ride an intentional v2 break.

4. **Matrix admin = `superAdmin` membership role only.** A new value
   is appended to `membershipRoleEnum` alongside `viewer | publisher |
   admin`. Reviewers decide their own assigned cells; matrix edits
   are a separate, smaller trust circle. Super-admins also bypass the
   cell-assignment check when deciding (so they can unblock a stuck
   request without first assigning themselves).

## Consequences

What this makes easier:

- The reviewer queue ranks naturally by cell and is small enough that
  super-admins can hold the whole matrix in their head.
- The CLI keeps working without any contract change — useful since
  the CLI is distributed via npm and slow to roll forward.
- The state machine is small enough to fit in
  `shared/approvals/state.ts` as pure functions with full unit
  coverage; the orchestrator (`server/utils/approvals.ts`) does
  nothing tricky beyond optimistic-lock race detection.

What we gave up:

- A single "officially endorsed" enum on `extensions` would have been
  one fewer column. The separate `officialTier` means we now have
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/naive/agentcenter-nuxt/docs/adr/0002-cli-extension-type.md",
  "content": "# ADR-0002: CLI as a fifth extension type\n\n## Status\n\nAccepted — 2026-06-10 (shipped via PR #52 on branch\n`feat/cli-extension-type`).\n\n## Context\n\nThe product needed a fifth `extension_category` value to cover\npublisher-uploaded CLI tools (executables, npm/uv packages, or\ncombinations) — a shape that didn't sit cleanly inside the four\nexisting categories (`skills`, `mcp`, `slash`, `plugins`). The\nshape of the change was open along four axes, each with plausible\nalternatives:\n\n1. **What is a \"CLI extension,\" semantically?** It could be an\n   executable installed onto `PATH`, a Claude-invokable tool living\n   under `~/.claude/cli/{slug}`, or an npm/uv package the AgentCenter\n   CLI installs on the user's behalf. Each carries a different\n   install model and a different blast radius.\n2. **How does it differ from `plugins`?** Claude Code plugins already\n   ship bundled scripts and binaries. The distinction needed to be\n   sharp enough that publishers don't have to guess which category\n   their work belongs in.\n3. **Is widening the `category` enum on the frozen `/api/v1`\n   contract a breaking change?** Older CLI binaries that\n   strict-validate the enum will reject `cli` rows; lenient clients\n   will simply skip unknown values.\n4. **Should the same PR also harden the installer's path-traversal\n   defense?** The new `cli.ts` installer was a verbatim copy of\n   `skills.ts`, which has a pre-existing path-traversal bug visible\n   to anyone reading the new file. Fixing only the new copy would\n   leave the original vulnerable while still satisfying the review\n   bot.\n\n## Decision\n\nFour locked decisions, taken together:\n\n1. **Payload is a directory drop into `~/.claude/cli/{slug}` —\n   PATH wiring stays the user's job.** The installer extracts the\n   uploaded zip into that directory and stops; whether the\n   contents are a single binary, an npm project requiring\n   `npm install`, or a Python package requiring `uv tool install`\n   is for the publisher to spell out via\n   `[install.<agent>.postInstall].message`. The marketplace does\n   not silently add things to `PATH` (blast-radius concern) and\n   does not run package managers on the user's machine (trust\n   concern). Users who want a PATH shortcut symlink it themselves\n   once.\n\n2. **The `cli` category sits alongside `plugins`, not under it.**\n   `plugins` is \"Claude Code plugin bundles\" (which Claude loads\n   automatically); `cli` is \"user-invoked tooling\" (which the user\n   runs directly from a shell). Same bundle shape, different\n   destination, different runtime — distinct enough that the\n   filter rail and the per-category landing pages stay legible.\n\n3. **Enum widening is additive, not a `/api/v1` break.** The\n   `category` enum gains `cli` via `ALTER TYPE … ADD VALUE`; no\n   existing field shape changes. The companion CLI binary must\n   ship in lockstep so users can actually install `cli` rows, but\n   the API itself is forward-compatible. Conventional Commits\n   reflect this — no `!` marker, no `BREAKING CHANGE:` footer.\n\n4. **The path-traversal hardening lands on both installers in the\n   same PR.** Slug validation and `resolveInside()` were extracted\n   into `cli/src/installers/safe-paths.ts` and wired through both\n   `skills.ts` and the new `cli.ts`. Fixing only the new file\n   would have left the pre-existing skills installer vulnerable;\n   fixing both keeps the two paths consistent so the next person\n   copying the installer pattern can't accidentally drop the\n   guard.\n\n## Consequences\n\nWhat this makes easier:\n\n- Adding a sixth or seventh extension type follows the same\n  template documented here: widen the enum (one migration),\n  update every type-level single source of truth, add an\n  installer with the right destination, drop catalog seeds, add\n  i18n labels. The fanout is mechanical because the surface area\n  is now exactly the surface area I had to touch this round.\n- Publishers who ship a CLI tool have an obvious home for it\n  instead of stuffing a binary into a `plugins` bundle and hoping\n  Claude doesn't try to load it as a plugin manifest.\n- Both installers now refuse zip entries that try to escape their\n  install root, so the worst a malicious bundle can do is\n  overwrite its own slug's directory — not someone else's\n  skill, plugin, or `~/.ssh/`.\n\nWhat we gave up:\n\n- The `ExtensionCategory` enum is now duplicated in **eleven**\n  places (one canonical type + one Drizzle pgEnum + four\n  validator z.enums + five local component duplicates). The PR\n  flagged this as tech debt; a follow-up should consolidate into\n  a single shared const that the validators import. Crossing the\n  shared/server/app/cli boundary makes the consolidation a\n  separate ADR-worthy change rather than something to bolt on\n  here.\n- The catalog seed (`shared/data/catalog.ts`) now carries 32 CLI\n  entries that have to be maintained as editorial content. The\n  alternative — letting publishers populate the category — would\n  have left a freshly-deployed Vercel environment with an empty\n  `/cli` listing and an empty CLI tab in `/admin/approvals`. The\n  editorial guard in `shared/data/catalog.test.ts` (lower bound\n  on entries spread across subCats) protects the commitment.\n- A user who wants their CLI tool on `PATH` has to run a one-line\n  `ln -s` after install. We chose \"explicit and inert\" over\n  \"convenient and surprising\"; making the default convenient\n  would have required either touching shell rc files, dropping\n  a wrapper into a system-managed `bin/`, or a privilege model\n  the marketplace doesn't have.\n\n## Notes\n\n- The `subCat` default for `cli` is `tools/cli` (see\n  `server/utils/publish.ts:defaultClassification`). This puts\n  uncategorized CLI submissions into a sensible bucket on the\n  taxonomy filter rail; publishers can override during publish.\n- The full implementation plan and per-commit split is preserved\n  at `/Users/hutusi/.claude/plans/i-am-thinking-of-merry-locket.md`\n  for the duration of this branch.\n"
}
```

  "old_string": "import { describe, expect, it } from \"vitest\"\nimport { CATALOG } from \"~~/shared/data/catalog\"\n\n// Guards the editorial decision that the home \"Recommended\" tab (which\n// filters `badge === 'official' AND category === 'skills'`) has enough\n// content to fill the grid. A casual badge edit could silently shrink the\n// tab back to a single row; this test makes that change loud.\ndescribe(\"catalog: official-skill curation\", () => {\n  const officialSkills = CATALOG.filter(\n    (e) => e.category === \"skills\" && e.badge === \"official\",\n  )\n\n  it(\"includes at least 12 official skills\", () => {\n    expect(officialSkills.length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(12)\n  })\n\n  it(\"spreads official skills across at least 3 functional categories\", () => {\n    const funcCats = new Set(officialSkills.map((e) => e.funcCat))\n    expect(funcCats.size).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(3)\n  })\n})",
  "new_string": "import { describe, expect, it } from \"vitest\"\nimport { CATALOG } from \"~~/shared/data/catalog\"\n\n// Guards the editorial decision that the home \"Recommended\" tab (which\n// filters `badge === 'official' AND category === 'skills'`) has enough\n// content to fill the grid. A casual badge edit could silently shrink the\n// tab back to a single row; this test makes that change loud.\ndescribe(\"catalog: official-skill curation\", () => {\n  const officialSkills = CATALOG.filter(\n    (e) => e.category === \"skills\" && e.badge === \"official\",\n  )\n\n  it(\"includes at least 12 official skills\", () => {\n    expect(officialSkills.length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(12)\n  })\n\n  it(\"spreads official skills across at least 3 functional categories\", () => {\n    const funcCats = new Set(officialSkills.map((e) => e.funcCat))\n    expect(funcCats.size).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(3)\n  })\n})\n\n// Guards the editorial decision (ADR-0002) that a freshly-deployed Vercel\n// environment lands with a populated /cli listing. The catalog seed runs\n// on every vercel-build via scripts/seed-catalog.ts, so cuts here are\n// what end users see; the lower bound is intentionally conservative\n// (20 of the 32 seeded) so editorial trimming has headroom without\n// triggering false alarms.\ndescribe(\"catalog: cli curation\", () => {\n  const cliTools = CATALOG.filter((e) => e.category === \"cli\")\n\n  it(\"includes at least 20 cli tools\", () => {\n    expect(cliTools.length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(20)\n  })\n\n  it(\"spreads cli tools across at least 3 subCats\", () => {\n    const subCats = new Set(cliTools.map((e) => e.subCat))\n    expect(subCats.size).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(3)\n  })\n\n  it(\"includes at least one official-tier cli tool per tier\", () => {\n    // Both Company and Product-Line official tiers should have visible\n    // examples so the filter rail's tier picker exercises real data on a\n    // fresh deploy.\n    const company = cliTools.filter((e) => e.officialTier === \"company\")\n    const productLine = cliTools.filter((e) => e.officialTier === \"productLine\")\n    expect(company.length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(1)\n    expect(productLine.length).toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(1)\n  })\n})"
}
